16:I[44692,["/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/08cykig6vtx_p.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/07qi2okp-.yp9.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0frujd.eyhz4o.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0n4z.7yu76je-.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/09-o.77-p0p36.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/1304s0wcp2kce.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0j1uy9t~zxcf0.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/14_5o-nbg8k_r.js"],"DailyView"]
17:T433,The technical implementation is not disclosed. The capability descriptions point to several design requirements. First, the input interface must fuse heterogeneous conditions: text instructions, image references, source videos, motion cues, visual style, and audio rhythm. Second, the current output is video, while later members of the Omni family are planned to support image and audio outputs, so the intended interface is broader than a standalone video generator. Third, multi-turn editing is treated as a primary mode rather than an add-on. Each instruction builds on the previous one, which requires persistent scene state and a mechanism for reconciling local edits with global consistency. Fourth, Gemini’s knowledge and reasoning are used to improve semantic faithfulness, for example in alphabet-item generation, protein-folding explainers, and physically plausible motion. Compared with conventional text-to-video systems, the distinctive point is the compositional conditioning space: existing media and conversation history are part of the generation problem.18:T427,Its value lies in the operational signals it provides. First, the token scale is large enough to matter for model design: from 9.7 trillion tokens per month two years ago, to roughly 480 trillion last year, to over 3.2 quadrillion per month now. At that scale, serving constraints and product usage patterns can feed back into what kinds of models are worth training. Second, Google explicitly separates TPU 8t for pretraining and TPU 8i for inference, which is a strong sign that training and inference co-design are diverging further. Third, the JAX plus Pathways plus multi-site training story suggests that the next bottleneck is no longer just a single datacenter fabric, but global scheduling, synchronization, fault tolerance, and data movement. Fourth, Gemini Omni points toward a unified multimodal generative direction: any input modality to any output modality, starting with video output and later extending to image and text. That has direct implications for tokenization, representation sharing, objective design, and modality mixing in pretraining.19:T640,This is an edited keynote transcript rather than a technical paper, so there is no reproducible method section. Still, the technical direction is clear. On infrastructure, Google introduces an eighth-generation TPU family with a dual-chip strategy: TPU 8t for large-scale pretraining, with nearly 3x the raw compute of the previous generation, and TPU 8i for inference, optimized around latency and efficiency. On the training-system side, Google says that with JAX and Pathways, training is no longer constrained to a single giant datacenter and can be distributed seamlessly across multiple sites, scaling to more than one million TPUs globally. That matters because it implies future pretraining recipes must tolerate more system heterogeneity and more complex synchronization topologies. On the modeling side, Gemini Omni is described as a model capable of generating any output modality from any input modality, with the first release, Omni Flash, starting from video outputs and later extending to image and text. This sounds closer to a unified generative framework than to a loosely coupled multimodal stack. At the product layer, Google highlights Search AI Mode, AI Overviews, Ask YouTube, Docs Live, and the Gemini app as examples of agentic interaction patterns that combine natural language, voice, retrieval, multimodal understanding, and task execution. For pretraining researchers, the key point is that these product demands will push models toward stronger long-context handling, lower-latency speech understanding, better cross-modal grounding, and more stable multi-turn behavior.1a:T42d,There is no conventional experimental section, but the scale metrics are informative. Google reports over 3.2 quadrillion tokens processed per month across its surfaces, roughly 19 billion tokens per minute through model APIs, more than 8.5 million monthly developers building with its models, and over 375 Google Cloud customers each processing more than one trillion tokens over the past year. On the product side, AI Overviews has over 2.5 billion monthly active users, AI Mode has surpassed 1 billion monthly active users, and the Gemini app has grown from 400 million MAU at the previous I/O to over 900 million, with daily requests up more than 7x. Image generation has exceeded 50 billion generated images. On infrastructure, TPU 8t is said to deliver nearly 3x the raw compute of the prior generation, while both 8t and 8i provide up to 2x better performance per watt. Multi-site training is framed as enabling training runs to shrink from months to weeks. These numbers are useful as system-scale indicators, not as controlled evidence for model-quality gains.1b:T48e,From a pretraining perspective, focus on four parts. First, read the token-scale and user-scale numbers carefully; they reveal changing optimization targets more clearly than the product language does. Second, study the TPU 8t/8i and JAX/Pathways discussion, because it speaks directly to the future systems boundary for large-scale training, especially cross-site training and the split between training and inference hardware. Third, pay attention to the Gemini Omni announcement. The details are sparse, but the direction is unambiguous: Google is pushing toward unified multimodal generation rather than treating multimodality as a bolt-on module. Fourth, look at product examples like Docs Live, Ask YouTube, and Search AI Mode. They show the actual I/O patterns the model must support at scale: voice, video, retrieval, long dialogue, and action in one loop. Estimated reading time is about 20 minutes. The two deeper questions worth carrying forward are how cross-site training changes optimizer, checkpointing, data ordering, and fault-tolerance design, and whether any-input-to-any-output models will force more unified tokenization and training objectives.1c:T4f4,The post outlines a workflow rather than a new objective:
- Inputs to normative specs: structured dialogues with religious/philosophical/humanist communities to inform documents like the Claude Constitution; explicitly aims for pluralism (equal depth across viewpoints) rather than adopting one tradition.
- Modeling premise: large-scale pretraining absorbs patterns of speaking/reasoning/choosing; subsequent training shapes which patterns are reinforced, effectively forming a “character”.
- Concrete mechanism experiment: add a mid-task callable tool that returns a short reminder of the model’s ethical commitments. The model learns/chooses to call it at consequential moments, often when it detects a conflict of interest.
- Open mechanism question acknowledged by the authors: how much of the gain comes from the reminder content vs. the act of pausing to reflect (a deliberation insertion). This matters because the latter could be implemented as a generic compute-time intervention, while the former is content/spec dependent.
- Trade-offs implied: shifting some alignment load from weights to external mechanisms improves controllability and iteration speed, but introduces reliance on tool availability and on the policy that decides when to invoke it.1d:T538,The announcement describes several prototypes. Hypothesis Generation is built with Co-Scientist. A researcher defines a research challenge, then a multi-agent “idea tournament” generates, debates, and evaluates hypotheses, with claims supported by clickable citations. The important design choice is iterative critique and evidence checking, not one-shot ideation. Computational Discovery is built with AlphaEvolve and ERA. It is described as an agentic research engine that generates and scores thousands of code variations in parallel, allowing researchers to explore modeling approaches for areas such as solar forecasting and epidemiology. Literature Insights is built with NotebookLM. It searches scientific literature, organizes results into tables with user-defined searchable attributes, supports grounded chat over a curated corpus, and produces artifacts such as reports, slides, infographics, and audio or video overviews. Science Skills connects more than 30 life-science databases and tools in Google Antigravity, including UniProt, AlphaFold Database, AlphaGenome API, and InterPro, so that agents can perform structural bioinformatics and genomic workflows. The overall pattern is a general model combined with tools, agent procedures, and verification mechanisms rather than a collection of narrow standalone models.1e:T425,The blog does not provide a unified benchmark, ablations, model sizes, or training data details. The validation evidence is mostly deployment-oriented and points to external papers. It states that ERA and Co-Scientist papers have been published in Nature, but it does not report their metrics. Partner examples include BASF using AlphaEvolve for supply-chain optimization, Klarna using it to improve machine-learning models, and Daiichi Sankyo, Bayer Crop Science, and U.S. National Labs under the Department of Energy’s Genesis Mission using Co-Scientist. For Science Skills, an internal early test reportedly reduced a complex analysis from hours to minutes and produced new clues about possible mechanisms for a rare genetic disease caused by AK2 mutations. The announcement also mentions validation with more than 100 institutions, including Stanford University, Imperial College London, and The Crick Institute, plus pilots with ICML, STOC, and NeurIPS for agentic peer review and scientific validation tools such as Paper Assistant Tool and ScholarPeer.1f:T672,The post does not disclose architecture, optimizer, data recipe, or training objective, so it should not be read as a full technical paper. Still, several design signals are clear. First, 3.5 Flash keeps the Flash identity of low latency, but the capability emphasis is on coding, agentic execution, and multimodal understanding. That usually implies training pressure on tool-use robustness, code editing, long-context retrieval, and cross-modal grounding, not just generic next-token prediction. Second, the repeated coupling with the Antigravity harness suggests the model is meant to operate under external orchestration, taking on planner, builder, player, or auditor roles and supporting parallel subagents. In practice, that requires model-side robustness to role conditioning, state tracking, and tool-feedback integration, plus system-side support for decomposition, context management, recovery, and supervision. Third, the safety section is notable: Google mentions stronger cyber and CBRN safeguards and interpretability tools that inspect internal reasoning before response generation. That points to a safety stack that likely goes beyond output filtering into intermediate-state monitoring or risk detection over latent reasoning traces. Fourth, the claim of roughly 4x output speed versus other frontier models, at often less than half the cost, implies co-design between model architecture and serving stack. Even without details, that kind of deployment target feeds back into pretraining choices such as token efficiency, sequence handling, and whether the model is optimized for dense high-throughput inference in agent workloads.20:T533,The available evidence is mostly benchmark reporting and product demonstrations rather than a controlled experimental section. Reported numbers include 76.2% on Terminal-Bench 2.1, 1656 Elo on GDPval-AA, 83.6% on MCP Atlas, and 84.2% on CharXiv Reasoning. Google states that 3.5 Flash outperforms Gemini 3.1 Pro on these coding, agentic, and multimodal benchmarks, while delivering about 4x the output tokens per second of other frontier models and often at less than half the cost. The showcased tasks are also informative: automatic renaming and categorization of unstructured assets, synthesizing the AlphaZero paper and building a playable game in six hours with two agents, migrating a legacy codebase to Next.js, generating city landscapes with parallel subagents, and running builder-player self-improvement loops for game development. Enterprise examples include Shopify for long-horizon data analysis, Macquarie Bank for reasoning over 100+ page onboarding documents, Salesforce Agentforce for multi-turn tool calling, Ramp for invoice OCR plus historical-pattern reasoning, Xero for multi-week 1099 workflows, and Databricks for monitoring, diagnosis, and remediation suggestions. These are directionally useful, but they are not substitutes for ablations, scaling curves, parameter counts, or training-token disclosures.21:T458,Read it as a capability signal, not as a methods paper. Three parts matter most. First, look at the benchmark selection: Terminal-Bench, GDPval-AA, MCP Atlas, and CharXiv together define Google's current capability target more clearly than the marketing language does. The emphasis is execution, coding, multimodal reasoning, and long-horizon reliability. Second, pay attention to Antigravity. Nearly every high-value example depends on the harness and subagent orchestration, which means the practical capability boundary is being redrawn around the combined model-plus-orchestrator system. That is the key lens for anyone thinking about what should be learned in pretraining versus delegated to runtime systems. Third, read the safety paragraph carefully. The mention of interpretability tools checking internal reasoning before response is more interesting than standard refusal language, and it is the part most likely to connect to future technical disclosures. Estimated reading time is 15 to 20 minutes. X Training: Feedback Conditioning Improves Scaling Across all LLM Training Stages","abstract":"We tackle the question of how to scale more efficiently across the many, ever-growing stages of current LLM training pipelines. for Science 解决的是科学研究流程中的代理化问题：如何让通用模型在文献理解、假设生成、计算实验和专业数据库调用中承担更长链条的研究任务，而不是只回答单个问题。","importance":"对预训练研究的影响不在新的语言建模目标，而在训练与评测接口。科学任务需要长上下文阅读、可靠引用、代码生成与执行、工具调用、多代理辩论、实验设计和结果验证。这些能力很难只靠普通网页语料学到，通常需要后训练、工具环境、可验证奖励和领域数据库接入。Gemini for Science 给出了一种可观察的系统形态：通用 Gemini 能力作为底座，Co-Scientist、AlphaEvolve、ERA、NotebookLM 和 Science Skills 负责把科学工作流拆成可执行、可检查的环节。对基座团队的启发是，科学能力不应只用问答 benchmark 衡量，更应放到带工具、带文献证据、带计算验证的闭环任务中评估。","technical_approach":"系统由几个原型工具组成。Hypothesis Generation 基于 Co-Scientist，流程是让研究者定义研究问题，然后通过多代理“idea tournament”生成、辩论和评估假设，并用可点击引用支撑关键主张。这里的重点不是单次生成，而是让候选假设经过竞争、批评和证据校验。Computational Discovery 基于 AlphaEvolve 和 ERA，定位为 agentic research engine，会并行生成和打分大量代码变体，用于探索新的建模或计算实验方案，例如太阳能预测和流行病学建模。Literature Insights 基于 NotebookLM，负责检索科学文献，把结果按用户自定义属性整理成表格，并在用户整理的语料范围内进行 grounded chat，同时生成报告、幻灯片、信息图、音频和视频概览。Science Skills 则把 30 多个生命科学数据库和工具接入 Google Antigravity，包括 UniProt、AlphaFold Database、AlphaGenome API 和 InterPro，用于结构生物信息学和基因组分析等多步骤工作流。和窄领域模型相比，这套方案更偏向“通用模型 + 工具 + 代理流程 + 验证”的组合。","experiments":"博客没有给出统一 benchmark 数字，也没有公开消融结果、模型规模或训练数据。给出的验证信息主要来自部署和外部合作。ERA 和 Co-Scientist 的研究论文已在 Nature 发表，但博客未列出具体实验指标。企业和科研合作包括 BASF 用 AlphaEvolve 优化供应链，Klarna 用其改进机器学习模型，Daiichi Sankyo、Bayer Crop Science 和美国能源部 Genesis Mission 相关国家实验室使用 Co-Scientist。Science Skills 的早期测试中，Google 研究团队把一个通常需要数小时的复杂分析缩短到数分钟，并获得了关于 AK2 基因突变导致罕见遗传病潜在机制的新线索。另有超过 100 家机构参与验证，包括 Stanford、Imperial College London 和 The Crick Institute；还提到与 ICML、STOC、NeurIPS 开展代理化审稿和科学验证工具试点，包括 Paper Assistant Tool 和 ScholarPeer。","reading_guide":"精读重点应放在任务分解和验证闭环。第一，看 Hypothesis Generation 的“multi-agent idea tournament”和引用校验设计，关注它如何降低 hallucination，并把开放式想法生成转成可审查流程。第二，看 Computational Discovery 中“生成并打分数千个代码变体”的描述，这接近可执行环境中的搜索与优化，可为 RL、程序合成和自动实验设计提供训练信号。第三，看 Science Skills 的数据库和工具接入方式，它说明高质量工具接口可能比把全部领域知识压进参数更有效。第四，看合作与验证段落，尤其是 Nature 论文、100 多家机构测试、会议审稿工具试点。需要注意，博客没有提供可复现实验数字；若要评估技术强度，应继续读 ERA、Co-Scientist、AlphaEvolve 及相关 Nature 论文。预计阅读博客 10 分钟，技术追踪需要转向关联论文和系统文档。","authors_note":"Google / Google DeepMind 发布。涉及 Co-Scientist、AlphaEvolve、ERA、NotebookLM、Google Antigravity、Science Skills Scientific agents require long-context reading, grounded citation, code generation and execution, tool use, multi-agent critique, experimental design, and result verification. These behaviors are hard to obtain from generic web-text pretraining alone. They usually need post-training, executable environments, verifiable rewards, and access to curated scientific databases. The system described here uses Gemini as the general model layer, with Co-Scientist, AlphaEvolve, ERA, NotebookLM, and Science Skills turning research workflows into structured and checkable steps. For foundation-model teams, the useful lesson is that scientific capability should not be measured only by static QA. agent 核心模型，同时不牺牲延迟与成本。Google 给出的答案是 Gemini 3.5 系列，先发布 3.5 Flash，目标是把“高智能”和“可行动”放在同一模型里，让模型不仅会答题，还能规划、调用工具、并行组织子代理、处理长文档和多模态输入，并在企业与消费级产品里直接落地。","importance":"对基座研究的意义主要有三层。第一，它把模型评价重心从传统静态 benchmark 明确推向 agentic workload，强调终局不是单轮问答，而是长时程执行、上下文保持、工具调用可靠性和多代理协作。第二，它说明小步快跑的高吞吐模型并不只是“便宜版旗舰”，而可以通过训练和系统协同，成为 agent 场景里的主力模型；这对预训练阶段的目标设计、数据配比和后训练接口都有直接启发。第三，它透露出一个重要趋势：模型能力不再只由参数规模定义，而是由模型本体、agent harness、子代理编排、以及安全与可解释性检查共同决定。对做 pretrain 的人来说，这意味着训练目标需要更早考虑 actionability，而不是把可执行性完全留给 post-train 或外部工具层。","technical_approach":"公开信息没有给出训练 grounding。其二，Google 反复强调与 Antigravity harness 的耦合，说明模型并非只做单代理对话，而是被设计成能在外部编排器下稳定承担 planner / builder / player / auditor 等不同角色，并支持并行子代理执行。这类能力通常依赖两部分：模型侧对角色切换、状态追踪、工具反馈整合的鲁棒性；系统侧对任务分解、上下文裁剪、失败恢复和监督执行的工程化支持。其三，文中提到“frontier safety framework”“更强的 cyber 与 CBRN safeguard”“用 interpretability tools 在回答前检查内部推理”，这说明安全训练不只是输出过滤，而可能包含更细粒度的中间表征监测或 reasoning trace 风险判别。其四，3.5 Flash 被描述为 4 倍于其他 frontier models 的输出速度，且成本低于一半，这暗示其推理栈、模型尺寸与 serving 优化是联合设计的；虽然不属于预训练细节，但会反过来约束模型架构和训练目标，例如更偏向高吞吐 dense 84.2%。文中称其在这些 coding、agentic 和 multimodal benchmark 上超过 Gemini 3.1 Pro，并且输出 token/s 约为其他 frontier models 的 4 倍，成本常低于其一半。案例层面，Google 展示了多个长时程任务：自动重命名和分类非结构化资产；用两个 agent 综合 AlphaZero 论文并在 6 小时内写出可玩的游戏；将遗留代码库迁移到 Next.js；并行生成城市景观；builder-player 自改进循环开发游戏；生成交互式动画、硬件原型和 UX 方案。企业侧案例包括 Shopify 用并行子代理做复杂数据分析，Macquarie Bank 处理 100+ 页文档做 onboarding，Salesforce 在 Agentforce 中做多轮工具调用，Ramp 做复杂发票 OCR 选择。Terminal-Bench、GDPval-AA、MCP Atlas、CharXiv 共同指向的不是传统聊天能力，而是代码执行、agent 可靠性、多模态推理和长任务完成度，这反映了 Google 当前对模型能力的内部目标函数。第二，看 Antigravity 的角色。文中几乎所有高价值案例都依赖 harness 和 subagents，这说明单模型能力边界正在被“模型+编排器”重新定义；做预训练的人需要思考哪些能力应在 base model 内生，哪些交给系统层。第三，看安全段落。Google 明确提到在回答前用 interpretability tools 检查内部推理，这比常见的输出后过滤更值得留意，后续如果有技术报告，优先关注安全训练、内部表征监测和 refusal calibration。阅读时间大约 风格的官方博客，未给出明确个人作者名单。"},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The central problem here is not generic model quality, but how to turn a frontier foundation model into a practical engine for long-horizon, tool-using, multi-step agent workflows without paying the usual latency and cost penalty. Gemini 3.5, starting with 3.5 Flash, is presented as Google's answer: a model family designed to combine strong reasoning, coding, multimodal understanding, and reliable action execution in real product environments.","importance":"For pretraining researchers, the significance is less about a disclosed algorithm and more about a shift in what counts as core model capability. First, the target has clearly moved from static QA-style evaluation toward agentic execution: long-horizon completion, tool use, context retention, and multi-agent coordination. Second, Google is signaling that a fast, lower-latency model can be the primary agent model rather than a cheaper fallback, which has implications for training objectives, data mixture, and the pretrain-to-posttrain interface.
